ParlaMint-AT_2017-06-29-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00190_d8e7272	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen. I believe that, first of all, when discussing the new sports promotion law and the arrangements behind it, we should also consider the starting point. The starting point is, on the one hand, both legal and competence-related, that sport is not only defined through the federal government and through the funding possibilities of the federal government, but, on the other hand, also very strongly through country competence. Of course, in the discussion you have to be careful not to confuse some competence. There is also an independence of sport, independence of sports organisations, BSO and other organisations. On the one hand, one has to take note of this as a minister, who may want to regulate one or the other, on the other hand, it is important that there is this autonomous sports world and that there is a certain division of competence – as in our federal system. On the other hand, it is also necessary to assess the starting position as it has shown itself at the federal level in the field of sports promotion grants. The starting point was for me – and so it was also presented to me by representatives from sports and sportsmen – that we currently have at federal level a sports promotion system in different areas with different approaches, with various possibilities to carry out with different modalities, bills – whether on the one hand in our home in the sports section, whether on the other side in the area of BSFF or through the RIO et cetera project. So there are really different approaches. I believe that it must be allowed to question this with fug and law – also in the sense of sportsmen – because we all want to make the best of the possibilities and possibilities for development in the field of sports for athletes. However, we also need to change these framework conditions accordingly. That is exactly what we have tried with this sports law of course. I also say quite openly that this is a first step. One will most likely be able to define development possibilities and further steps again and again. These ways must also be followed in the future, but this is a first important step in order to provide a clear line here. The clear line is clear: All the funding we are awarded at the federal level, including the possibilities of the house, also my possibilities to grant funding, are transferred to the GmbH according to clear criteria and guidelines. There will also be clear criteria and guidelines for the controllers in the area of this GmbH. If we implement a daughter in this area in the form of this GmbH, this does not mean that politics stems from responsibility. Furthermore, politics must, because it is public funds and funds, now it must also define what the objectives are to be achieved. The objectives are clear from my point of view: On the one hand, we urgently need harmonisation in the field of sports facilities, especially between the federal government and countries. We need a clear commitment to the fact that the sports associations – many are very well managed – are also developing correctly that they are well managed that the associations, which are the home of the top athletes, are also a good basis for the top athletes. We have clearly stated that there must be a different accounting model so that associations can also plan for several years within the meaning of the athletes, a budget allocation that is not based on the fact that every association has to ask itself again and again whether it needs to pre-finance accordingly, which projects it needs to submit in order to re-transform projects in order to actually use the funds for what it needs. There is and will also be a corresponding advance of trust for the work of the associations, but – and this is clear from my point of view – there must also be a control and controlling system behind it and there must be a clear commitment to the fact that there is a principle of performance in the area of the management of the associations and then, when it comes to sport and the development of sports. We may also need to think of other ways in the field of broadband, we must think in regions, we must work together between the umbrella organisations, we must also force joint projects. This too will be a clear goal of this GmbH for the promotion of sports. If I may be able to judge individual opinions or individual speeches here, I say that it is important to me that we also talk about the things that are actually included in the law. I already said it today: If one speaks of the fact that the representatives of sport, the associations responsible, may be able to determine in the Commissions themselves how, at what level and whether funding is granted, then this is simply not correct. Rather, an agreement must be reached in an interplay between management and the Commission. If no agreement can be reached, the decision will be taken in the Supervisory Board, which will subsequently have to be decided by a majority of three quarters. If the decision cannot be made in this way and in this way, then there is no grant. This also shows quite clearly that the controller who sits in the Commission cannot finally determine whether he can receive support in this way and scale. In addition, there is also an inconsistency scheme that when it comes to the question of its association being involved in the funding question, the respective member of the association must not get to the point and not vote for it at all. On the question of transparency: It is right to note that already in the BSFF there was a possibility of transparency, a possibility to publish, but not in other areas. We have now summarized all federal funds, and all federal funds are made transparent in the way they are granted to whom they are granted. If we are discussing a new review procedure today, for which this request has been made, I have to ask myself whether this law has not been followed in recent months. We have gone quite clearly and consciously a different way than in the creation and creation of laws. We have spent months talking to trade unions and umbrella associations. The law was created in its configuration in this circle of the sports world. ParlaMint-AT_2017-06-29-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00190_d8e10491	Next speaker: Mr Keck. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2017-06-29-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00190_d8e10512	Mr President. Federal Minister! I am trying to get back to the subject area, and I would like to refer to Agenda item 3. Ladies and gentlemen! According to the current legal situation, employees were able to acquire tax-advanced shares in the company of the service provider, which was up to an amount of €3 000 per annum tax-advanced. This regulation was extremely inflexible, which is why we will now adopt an employee participation funding law. With the introduction of this employee participation funding law, we are really putting in place important national economic measures that will further strengthen and strengthen Austria's economic location. The main objective is to provide a legal framework enabling employees to better implement their participation in a company. In particular, the purpose and purpose of the employee participation fund is to create or strengthen a core shareholder and thus to avoid a hostile takeover of an Austrian company as well as securing jobs at the respective location. For this purpose, the shares are to be faithfully kept and managed by the employee participation foundation for the employees. The advantage of this loyal administration is a uniform exercise of voting rights and the assurance of the employee participation foundation as a core shareholder. In order to be able to fulfil the role as a core shareholder as quickly as possible and to ensure a basic allocation of the employee participation fund with shares within the meaning of a relevant equity stock, the employee participation fund itself should be able to hold additional shares in the company, which has not been possible so far. However, these shares must be gradually handed over to the employees and then held loyally by the employee participation foundation. However, the number of shares that this employee participation fund itself may hold is limited by 10 percent of the voting rights in the company. The shares are to be left by employees in the employee participation foundation for loyal administration and custody on a permanent basis. Ladies and gentlemen! Such a construction has the employee participation foundation of voestalpine, which was built in 2000. The two largest single shareholders of voestalpine, the employee participation foundation and the Raika Oberösterreich, together hold 30 percent. This is the guarantee that this company will remain in Upper Austria, keep its location in Austria and secure jobs for its employees. Therefore, it is important to decide on this employee participation funding law – important for the employees, important for the Austrian site and important for the economy in Austria.

These institutions are partly financed from backflows and partly from recoverys, because some of these loans are subsidies. It is also good money because, apart from the objectives, two essential effects are achieved. This is, on the one hand, a pooling of resources. To mention only the example of the IDA: Over the next three years, the IDA will have available an amount of 75 billion dollars, of which 27 billion are made available from donor countries and a large part comes from return flows. For the first time, the IDA will also issue its own bonds. The second point is not only an effect of pooling, but also the lifting of private money. The contribution of Austria is fairly significant: Our share of the African Development Fund is 2.2 percent, our share of the International Development Organisation is 1.6 percent, so Austria is well positioned there. I'm looking for broad approval. Thank you.

May 2018 enters into force and is therefore intended to enter into force the Data Protection Adjustment Act so that the standard recipients can prepare themselves in time and in scope for this law because it represents a very extensive change of the entire data protection area. However, most of the criticisms of content do not concern our law at all, but the basic data protection regulation, which was adopted at European level and which is in principle directly applicable. We have also not taken them into the law literally, as it applies on its own to all European countries. At the same time, however, a directive has been adopted which requires its own transposition for the parts of the internal and judicial sector, and a large part of the legislation adopted is the implementation of this Directive. The other parts of this implementation concern the authorities themselves, the legal path, the liability and other measures to be regulated at national level. That is to say, the content part is essentially defined by the European Union General Data Protection Regulation. It is only the formal part of the implementation of the authorities and the implementation of the directive. In both parts of the implementation, which corresponds to the national area, we have referred to the fact that there are also specific exceptions. Where there are opening clauses, We have made use of it rich in science and also in images, and in the end we have met all the criteria. The criticism points are, as always, in such an area: It is too little data protection for the data protectors, the companies or businesses it is too much data protection. Those who have complained in particular are the secret services that say it will be much more difficult because of the fact that the data is of course better protected, including in the area of law enforcement and the prosecution authorities. In the future, there will be an obligation to delete, i.e. when the procedure is set up, the addressee will have to be informed and the data collected will have to be deleted. In essence, this is a very moderate, carefully implemented integration of the General Data Protection Regulation and transposition into national law, and it ultimately meets all the requirements. If you look at the one on the one hand, which feels too little used, and the other on the other, which feels too much used, then you will probably have proved the right eye measure with the mean measure. I therefore believe that this is a proper, timely and manageable implementation of this basic Regulation.

This has even gone so far as to call for the decoupling of simple legislators and constitutional legislators, since the simple legislator has always tried to use this instrument of power, namely the constitutional laws, in an inflationary sense. What I want to say here is: We should take our noses and not make too much use of this instrument, and if we do so, in a coherent way, so that we can read the Constitution so that we also know what is actually inside it. ParlaMint-AT_2017-06-29-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00190_d8e13985	Ladies and gentlemen! Sometimes parliamentary debates are great battles, and sometimes there are great legal details that we are discussing here, but it is also necessary to discuss these legal details, and I appreciate, for example, Mr Vetter of the ÖVP, that he is doing this with dedication. But I'm trying to translate this for our listeners a little: What's this about? . We need to adopt an additional protocol to the European Convention on Human Rights, and this Additional Protocol amends the Constitution because the European Convention on Human Rights is in the constitutional order. Now we have the following legal problem: The government parties have made a proposal which would have meant that this additional protocol should be adopted by a simple majority, although it amends constitutional law, and that the decision then takes two-thirds by majority in the Constitution. That's an unsatisfying solution. Why? – Because the situation could arise that the additional protocol would be approved by a simple majority, we will be obliged to do so by international law, but no two-thirds later will come together. That is why, from our point of view, this procedure was constitutional and parliamentaryly wrong. We have therefore made another proposal, and this one says that there must first be the constitutional basis – which is to be decided by a majority of two thirds – and then one can decide on the additional protocol with a majority of two-thirds, perhaps by a simple majority, because then the constitutional basis stands and we do not get the problem that we are then bound by international law, but perhaps do not have a majority. So, that's what we're doing now, and that's right; and why I think that simultaneousity isn't going, has a simple reason: Because, of course, the constitutional legal basis must first also acquire legal power – after the Federal Council and the Federal President were also involved. Only when the constitutional basis is established can the additional protocol be adopted in a second step. That is all very legal, but for this reason the simultaneousity is not actually in question and is the procedure we have chosen, the legal and constitutionally clean solution. This was important to us, because such a thing always has a prejudicial effect on the future. However, I am glad that in this very dry matter, we agreed to choose this procedure, but this does not free us from talking about it at ease – what you, Mr Vetter, have been talking about – as we are dealing with in the future, so that the Constitution has a certain degree of consistency, legibility and clarity. I wouldn't be that far away from you right now. As a solution then looks, you need to discuss in peace. - Thank you.

[[14:41:57]] 19. Point Report by the Constitutional Committee on Amendment No 2227/A by Dr Peter Wittmann, Mag. Wolfgang Gerstl, colleagues in a federal law amending the eGovernment Act (1765 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2017-06-29-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00190_d8e15109	We're coming to 19. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Magnificent came to speak. Stefan. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2017-06-29-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00190_d8e15136	Mr President. Mrs. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! The agenda item 19 is about amending the so-called eGovernment law. Austria is thus very far ahead of many other countries. It is also welcome that we are developing this legal matter here. This law today creates a so-called electronic identity card. It is intended to replace the citizen card and the mobile signature; a basically correct step. However, we will vote against this law today, because we criticise some of the points we have made in response to the fact that this law has not been sufficiently discussed and therefore our entries and comments have not been taken into account. I hope that this can be partially repaired in the post-amendum, and will therefore also bring in a resolution on this subject. Now to the criticism points: The certificates and identity-building data now collected for each citizen are held and managed by so-called trust service providers. These are private companies. I am convinced that such a sensitive process as the management of these data is a fundamental act. So it is a key task of the state to manage such things. It is therefore wrong to transfer the management of such data to private companies. And even if you say, After the control of the Ministry of the Interior or something else!, it is nevertheless fundamentally wrong, because this is a typical critical infrastructure if you want to, and therefore it should be managed by the state. – This is the first criticism point. The second criticism is: Such an electronic process always has the problem of logging things. That means, it's understandable what's going on there. And that is why, when you do it properly, you usually do it with a double coding, by anonymizing it twice, so that it is not understandable who has asked at any time and therefore has moved where. For example: A young person wants to enter a discotheque and want to prove what age he has. Then, in the Ministry of the Interior, there is no need to show up a record where it is said that Mr Sowieso was on the sound so many at this discotheque and has asked for a question. This is something that is not willing and which is not meaningful. We discussed this in committee, and it seems that we did not understand what I meant by it. Then: A person from the Federal Chancellery has rightly explained to us that it is just twice anonymized and therefore this protocol is not made. I also think it might be done that way, but the law doesn't contain it. It is not in the law that the technical conditions are created that such a logging does not take place. That's what I want. I want it to be in the law, because if it is actually made different, then you could change it at any time under the law and do such a logging. It's supposed to be inside. My motion for a resolution, which I shall make immediately, is also intended to do so. The third criticism is: The law itself provides for a voluntaryity. That means, as a citizen, I don't need such an electronic identification, but I can have it. I'll get him voluntarily or I'll have him voluntarily. However, the fact is that in the future, when someone requests a passport, he will automatically receive such an identification unless he opposes it. That is, in reality, if you want a passport, you get a form, fill it out, and once you have given a mobile phone number, you automatically have an ID. That's so classic: One can opt out, but in reality that doesn't happen. I don't know if there's any teaching of the citizens at all, either, by saying: You don't have to, that's voluntary, but I think it'll just run out automatically. But if something is to be voluntary, then it has to run reverse, then you have to opt in and expressly explain that you want it, and not vice versa. We want these three points to be changed. The most important thing, however, is that this logging is technically impossible. Therefore, I make the following request: [[Quoted print version removed]] I would ask that this request be accepted. Its content is reasonable. With this motion for a resolution, we want to create a framework for the law which is likely to be adopted now, and I hope today that the discussion will continue and that we will at least agree on the need for these technical conditions.
ParlaMint-AT_2017-06-29-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00190_d8e15225	The motion for a resolution is sufficiently supported, properly implemented, and is therefore under negotiation. The application reads as follows: [[Quoted print version removed]]

ParlaMint-AT_2017-06-29-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00190_d8e15322	Mr President. Mrs. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! A digital security partnership between citizens, the economy and the administration is what we call for in Government 4.0, and this is also what is an efficient development for us in the future, including in the administration, where reform is always called for. But the top priority must be that it must be user-friendly and that it must comply with technical safety. The aim is to promote digitalisation, to open up potential for citizens much more and to create opportunities for the economy. We have already passed laws here that we have required the economy to be able to deal with their business online by 2023 and to ensure compliance with personal data security. The comprehensibleness, which you have now criticised, Mr Stefan, is given by this protocol. There is a transparent protocol, and you can see exactly who, when and for what has been looked for and what data has been passed on. This eGovernment law, which is item 19 of the agenda, is intended to provide the legal basis for an electronic identity card and, above all, the possibility for citizens to demonstrate their identity safely on the network. We need an adjustment insofar as the whole is linked to two requirements. On the one hand, there is the eIDAS Regulation at European level, where we need to align, because we want a national solution, but also a trans-regional one for the whole of Europe. There is also the advantage for the economy that things are recognised cross-border, and this can only be beneficial. This has to be created in the end, and we are laying the foundation building block for it today. On the other hand, one needs a secure, modern and digital identity management for this technical segment, because there should be the possibility of doing things about the office, but no longer has to go to the office, but the office can be sure that it is really the person who has requested the data. There are, I think, the security features are very successful. This new eID card will only be issued to the public authorities, which are currently the passport authority and the national police inspection. And what changes? – You said it yourself: If you request a passport, you don't have to take the eID card, you can opt out. There is no obligation to do so, and so as a citizen and a citizen, there is a free choice. I believe that this is a good way to the digital future, and I believe that it is also a safe way. Of course, we also need to accompany this whole thing with further measures, we can also evaluate it, and I am convinced that technical development will also progress so that we may have to adapt it again, but I think it will be a good electronic progress.
ParlaMint-AT_2017-06-29-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00190_d8e15368	Next speaker: Mrs Maurer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2017-06-29-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00190_d8e15389	Mr President. Mrs. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! In principle about this e-ID story: We all know that, we all have too many accounts with too many passwords, which we are too happy to forget, which do not comply with the safety standards – I would guess now – which are actually necessary, and exactly this chaos is to be eliminated. I assume you're the same as me. I lost the overview. I don't have an overview of where I have an account anywhere and which password I always use for it, I rely on the memory function of the browser or my password manager. But precisely for this very question, of course, the establishment of an electronic identification certificate is, in principle, very helpful and should also be welcomed. However, from our point of view, the present draft law presents some major problems. I would like to say in principle: What is necessary to prove the electronic identity is that it is centralized through an identity management system, and the fundamental problem is always: Is the user behaviour comprehensible, yes or no? It is a problem, of course, if, for whoever, even if these are only two people, for example, it is understandable that you first bought something on an online platform and then anything in an electrical trade or that you might have been in a disco, as Mr Stefan said as an example. Of course, that can't be. There must be no way to monitor citizens, and this is not sufficiently guaranteed in this law. It is true, as Mr Stefan said: Yes, we had this teaching by an official in the committee who promised us that there was a double anonymization, but we cannot rely on it. Either it's in the law or it's not in it, and the latter means that you're in Undoubtedly as a citizen cannot resist it if the user's behaviour can actually be viewed by someone. This is a fundamental problem, and this law does not provide for any way to deal with it and how to exclude it. For this reason, we Greens will not agree to this bill. There are, however, a few other points to be criticized. First: The master number registry authority is established with the data protection authority and should there be data protection violations, then its own authority must control it. This is not good, it is a control conflict that we should, in principle, exclude in our laws. Secondly, A photo will also be stored in the dataset, and this poses a very serious danger in particular with regard to automated camera monitoring with biometric data. We Greens do not want there to be data acquisitions and data storage there and in the end become possible abuse and monitoring. The Greens will not agree to this bill. My appeal is to make clear in these important points. We will make our contribution to this in the next GP, but today we will not go along with this law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2017-06-29-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00190_d8e15459	Mr President. Mrs. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! On the one hand, the eGovernment law requires adaptation due to the need for the implementation of European legislation, and on the other hand, in the sense of a secure, modern, digital identity management, the citizen card is further developed into a comprehensive electronic identity card. The aim is to increase the use of the citizen card and facilitate its use in other European countries. This requires not only a new registration process, but also an adapted technical solution. In addition, the necessary legal framework for the use of notified electronic identifiers from other EU countries will also be established for Austrian online services. Despite the changes mentioned above, the proven function of the Austrian citizen card, in particular in its expression as a mobile signature, remains in principle. However, electronic identification will have much more scope for use. This results, on the one hand, from the future legal recognition in the other EU Member States, on the other hand, from the use of the electronic identification certificate, not only the core identity data, i.e. first name, family name, date of birth, but also data from administrative registers can be transmitted. This concerns, for example, driver's license and registration data or citizenship certificates. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! If you look through the opinions on amending the eGovernment Act, you can find that of 26 opinions received, only two are very critical. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Maurer.]] In many opinions, it is noted positively that the State and non-private providers have to fulfil these tasks, as security is better guaranteed by the state. It is also repeatedly stressed that this service is a demonstration project when it comes to the State performing its role as a security service provider for citizens. The Data Protection Council has noted that electronic identification is a necessity. To this end, it should also be stressed that this system corresponds to all national and international data protection provisions as before. Ladies and gentlemen! We are in a state of law with high security and data protection standards, where citizens can rely on a state's law-compliant behaviour. It should also be stressed that the State does not have access to data that it does not already have in the context of existing registers and on the basis of existing legal bases. The administration shall: only a secure infrastructure for the transmission of state-owned data is available to citizens. These are exclusively in existing state registers. Ladies and gentlemen! With your consent, ensure that a single framework for electronic identity management is provided and that the regulatory framework for handling electronic identity certificates is ensured! Thank you.

Flexibility is seen negatively in many areas, while, if it is appropriate for workers and employers, it can be a positive way of working together. In Switzerland, one has chosen the way for six out of ten employees to work flexibly, according to the model of annual working time. There is a traffic light system where you know as an employee when it lights up red, you have too few hours when it is green, then you have overtime, and the employee can make a concrete decision with his employer and determine in advance whether this is paid in the form of money, in the form of holiday time or in any case. This flexible opportunity, namely that employers and employees talk together and find the optimal solution for themselves, is a way that I also wish for Austria, because I assume that the companies and their employees know what requirements they need to meet, and how they can create it together – and the emphasis on it together – That is why we are also in favour of employee participation in order to create more in-house cooperation and to go a common path of workers and employers into a positive future. Thank you.

A special performance, however, is the construction of the whole, which is ideal for abuse. [[a panel on which a graphic can be seen, placing on the speaker's desk]] I would like to give you an example: Imagine a company with 200 employees and ten rental workers. The employment status is 200 because only 200 employees are registered with social security. The ten rental workers are reported to the deceased. Now, as a company often has, this company has 5 percent fluctuations, i.e. ten employees in one year. These are the ones that are marked red on this graphic. [[referring to the panel on the speaker's desk]] Now this company takes over the temporary workers in the permanent state of employment, then you have 200 employees again. But now the company has to replace the ten exits and thus hire ten new employees. The employment status is now 210. Although they did not create such a job, they have ten eligible jobs. That is, in fact, an industrial promotion. For this reason, the industrial association has also given such great importance that this process can be supported in the form. You can't even control whether someone who does this has bypassed the system on this path. Of these € 2 billion, not much will land in actually new jobs, but these are takeover effects. It is an industrial promotion that the EIA absolutely wanted and in which the SPÖ hops with it so that it can roast its 780 million for the 20 000 action. What the economy really needed would be a comprehensive tax relief, a comprehensive relief of additional labour costs, instead of a bureaucratic process for the companies that, if they employ people, have to send a lot of documents to the aws and then, for the sake of compassion, to the A state that has cleared it up will receive a promotion. - Thank you.

(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Ladies and gentlemen, The law of economic loyalty has rules and provisions in a very sensitive environment. It is about responsibility, it is about care, and it is about protecting professional groups with long and challenging training. Therefore, precisely here the balance of interests is the highest ceiling. In particular, the training of auditors and tax advisors must be developed forward-looking and at the same time serve to ensure the level of the profession in a sustainable manner. In addition, it is mainly about administrative efficiency, legal certainty and, of course, also legal protection for our small and medium-sized entrepreneurs, as well as for individual companies in Austria, who do not have a company attorney for all taxes and business matters, but usually only their tax advisor as first contact person, legal advisor and representative. That is why we have now provided for some extension of the rights in the overall reform of the law. I think it is really a good compromise between the different professional groups. On the one hand, it ensures the extension of rights, on the other hand takes account of the scope of the rights of other professions and, of course, makes use of some compromise. It is also a real added value for the customers of the auditors and tax advisors, because this makes many services and consulting services possible from a single source. I think that is a very crucial point. The new regulation of this law on economic loyalty is a kind of general resurgence of the law and an adaptation to the current events and requirements of our economic life. The innovations concern in particular three areas: It is very important to implement the money laundering directive as well as adapt vocational training and the already mentioned enlargements and clarifications of professional powers. ParlaMint-AT_2017-06-29-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00190_d8e26160	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! Values Advisors! I may receive the President of the Chamber of Economic Trustees. [[Congratulations with the FPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] I am referring to the business loyalty law 2017 and also to the Accounting Act 2014. Art. 3 Z 4 lit. ==References== Money laundering directive defines the expression of the criminal activity. This provision provides for an option as regards the definition of criminal activity. Either to the maximum extent of punishment or to the minimum penalty. The comparison with other countries, such as Germany or Switzerland, shows that not all tax offences are flat-rate and that not all are subject to the threat of punishment alone. Fortunately, at the last minute, an agreement on the definition of the money laundering act was reached in § 87 para. 2 Z 2 WTBG 2017 and § 43 para. 2 Z 2 Accounting Act 2014 based on the German Regulation. Otherwise, as a money laundering act, crimes would also have been committed which had nothing to do with the objectives of the money laundering directive, namely the fight against money laundering and terrorist financing. The tax advisor would have been obliged, in accordance with the old definition of the money laundering act in accordance with § 96 WTBG2017, to report all tax offences to the money laundering service. If the tax advisor did not report, he would have been fined. This would have been equally applicable to the balance sheet account holders in accordance with § 52a of the 2014 Accounting Act. The tax advisor and the accountant would have been financial police assistants in the future. Thank God a directive-compliant solution was found together with the Ministry of Justice and the Ministry of Economic Affairs. I am particularly pleased that we can still give the Chamber of Economic Trustees the opportunity to be appointed in the Chamber of Tax Advisors and Auditors, in short KSW. On this occasion, I would like to thank the chairman of the Committee on Economic Affairs Peter Haubner, but also Mr Matznetter and all those involved for the constructive cooperation when these two laws come into being. ParlaMint-AT_2017-06-29-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00190_d8e26720	Mr President. Federal Minister! It is about the eco electricity law or, by the way, the small eco-stromnovel, which all parliamentary parties have been working for months. I must say that on the part of the liberals we have only been involved on the verge of the negotiations. Although we have been informed, we were not – how should I say? – directly involved in the negotiations. Why do I say that? – Because we have received the amendment proposal – and this will happen to Mr Brunner as many representatives of opposition parties know – only one and a half hours ago. [[Abg. Brunner: I informed you before!]] – Some information was already available, but in the end I received the amendment one and a half hours ago. We have now also been reported as being counter to this item on the agenda. I have to say, however, that we are probably going to do so, and I would also like to explain that. For us, energy policy is always in a balance, in a balanced triangular relationship between renewable energy, energy transition on the one hand and durability, i.e. the price situation to be seen on the other side, and on the other hand, security of supply or network security, of course, also plays a role. These three parameters must always be taken into account within the framework of a reasonable energy policy. For us, taking these three parameters into account, the small ecostromnovelle was important that there is no additional cost for the end customers. One thing is clear: The green electricity regime is ultimately paid by the end-users via the corresponding levy. Who are the end customers? – These are households, but of course they are small and medium-sized enterprises, and this is also the industry, with about 65 percent paid by households and small and medium-sized enterprises. We have already heard a lot about location policy, price policy, and price control. That was a criterion for us. When we talk about the small ecostrom novels, of course, this is related to the amount of funding in the next few years. There we have, I must honestly say, no valid data. That means that our decision-making base is a little bit in the fog. What exactly is it about? – The four major areas of renewable energy are biogas, wind, photovoltaics and water. According to our level of information: The promotion of biogas was known. There is now an improvement in it for us, as the efficiency rate has been increased and the maximum proportion of food that is burned has been defined. The funding volume was also known: It is about 160, 162 million €, of course distributed over the next few years. Wind is strongly promoted, 45 million € times 13, so we're talking about 600 million €. In the field of photovoltaics, for me, the clear direction of investment promotion, technology promotion is an improvement. And the hydropower – there is a backlog, we know – has been taken into account accordingly. For us, it was important that – and I am therefore on the third side of the triangle, security of supply, network security – in parallel with the expansion of renewable energies ideally also the expansion of the corresponding infrastructure must take place. Measures, laws and regulations that make this expansion of infrastructure more difficult by way of a fee are a major danger – see, for example, Germany! – for the success of the transition to renewable energies. There are ideas such as strategic environmental impact assessment, a strengthening of the Water Law Directive, a strengthening of the exemptions from the table, and this is good. Of course, there are also a few things inside that actually have nothing to do with eco-strom. We all know: There the coalition partners have obviously also been calmed down, keyword CHP promotions and counting point summary, i.e. greetings to the Vienna energy. Policy is always a matter of compromises. For us liberals, this is quite portable as an overall package, which is why, apart from our message as a counter-redner, we will also agree to this small eco-strom novel. Nevertheless, I would like to say that, in the next legislative period, we must make the big eco-electric novel or even better a renewable energy law, which has been learned from the mistakes of the time of the eco-electric regime since 2003. In particular, we need to ensure more market-compliant behaviour of providers. I do not believe that this can be done further in such a way that completely market-independent fixed tariffs – which, one has to say, for example, in wind at 15 cents the five times the market price – are guaranteed. We will not support the continued implementation of the regime. It is therefore more likely to go away from direct subsidies to fiscal advantages and indirect subsidies such as less turnover tax and cetera. A clear directive: Fossil resource consumption should be treated differently from renewable energy – only as a small prospect of the large eco-electric innovation in the next legislative period. As has already been said: In the context of an overall assessment, we will be able to agree to this law.

It is precisely during this period that companies have to calculate very, very well, and often at this stage they do not decide the company's idea of whether or not a trademark can come to the patent office or not, but the financial possibilities. It is precisely because, I am convinced, the law is in place, because the processes of protecting a brand in the future will clearly be better financed for start-ups and SMEs. To date, for example, the renewal fees had been staggered and with every extension of the brand a new renewal fee had to be paid. This is now being re-regulated, the renewal fee for individual brands will be €700 in future, no matter how many renewals there have been. In the case of association brands, there will be 1 300 € in the future, no matter how many renewals there have been. For comparison: To date, fees of €2 715 were due for the first extension, €3 133 for the second extension and €3 551 for the third extension. I think that's a positive decision. The reason for this positive change is, as has been said, an EU directive, which now benefits our companies in national implementation. I think that is more a reason to agree to this implementation of the EU Directive, which we will also be very pleased to do. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2017-06-29-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00190_d8e29521	Madam President. Federal Minister! The Federal Government's work programme from 2013 to 2018 states that there should be a economical and efficient reorganisation of transport safety agents and a pooling of resources. The Federal Ministry has commissioned a project for the implementation of this government mandate, which was completed in September 2015 and finally approved by the department management. In the field of shipping supervision, it has been proposed in the course of this project that the operational activities currently located in the BMVIT should be outsourced. From now on, as mentioned several times, these activities are to be continued and carried out by via donau. The Via donau is a distributed legal entity of the Ministry, so that a much stronger focus can be placed in the Ministry itself on the supervisory activities in the shipping supervision. The operational activities allocated are mainly carried out in the context of cross-border shipping and water markings. In concrete terms, this means the procurement, construction, maintenance, maintenance and removal of these markings of the waterways, which are carried out by the via donau. However, the work will continue to take place under the arrangement and supervision of shipping supervision, including the nominated responsibility and responsibility of these shipping police duties in the field of shipping supervision. This transfer of the work leads to savings of € 110 000 annually resulting from reduced material and material costs. We see it is a sensible law, and of course I also hope for your approval.

ParlaMint-AT_2017-06-29-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00190_d8e29579	Madam President. Dear ladies and gentlemen! I would like to address some of the issues raised. The high costs for the via donau at the beginning are explained by the fact that it is now necessary to replace the entire tonne and bake material – if these terms are true. We still have metal and plastic tons, which limit the waterways, but which not only limit the waterways, but also serve as a danger sign and similar, and are necessary in order to be able to navigate safely on the Danube. But time does not stop, even in navigation it does not stop, and the visibility navigation with watermarks is now slowly, but safely replaced by digital navigation. In other words, it is necessary to replace these watermarks with plastic watermarks that have inside GPS receivers and make it possible for the passing ships to recognize the watermarks by means of modern tracking methods even if they are zero. That's the part that concerns the waterways. As far as the Federal Institute for Transport is concerned, I would like to thank everyone who has signaled support. Let me say one thing: I have not dealt with public statements, I have dealt with the request, Mr President, and the request is quite clear in his text. I have had the application examined legally by relevant awarding rights experts. These rights experts have concluded: The application is first of all legally indispensable because everything relating to the award of railway services is regulated in the PSO and therefore there is no need to deal with the contract law. Secondly, the application is legally missing because the content is not in accordance with the heading. The heading includes railway undertakings, but the text refers to all carriers referred to in the PSO Regulation, but the PSO Regulation also mentions buses. So it does not agree with headline and text. [[Abg. Fekter: But this is word-claubery!]] – We lawyers now examine this more precisely. The text is also harmful and illegal in Europe. For example, the PSO Regulation lays down that emergency allocations are feasible and possible if there are any major problems. The application also excludes such emergency payments and also calls for tenders in the event of an emergency. The fifth point I noticed is the use of the concept of local railway in the application; however, the term local railway does not exist in the PSO regulation or in the Austrian Railway Act, so it is unclear what is meant by it. This would allow the conclusion that not only overland connections, far-reaching connections are meant, but also regional transport, trams and subways. For these reasons, Mr Ottenschlager, I am opposed to this request. I believe that a regulation that prohibits the federal, state and local authorities from deciding for themselves whether they want to sign out or whether they want to make a direct award would make these institutions unmistakable. With such a request you will certainly not agree with me. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2017-06-29-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00190_d8e31033	Madam President. Dear Mrs. Volksanstalt! Mr. Volksanwälte! High house! We have 40 in committee. Report submitted by the Public Prosecutor's Office and presented it sufficiently. In view of the late hour, I would like to limit myself to cases in the chapter on family and youth, because there is a ten-page argument on how to deal with the problem of cross-border family benefits. We have heard the word "social abuse" here in the hall a few times today. That's a little different. What is the real problem? – A child is born in the world, but parents are from different EU countries. According to EU law, the country is obliged to work for family benefits, i.e. childcare and family allowances, in which father or mother is employed. This is, in principle, a reasonable solution, which is necessary for the normal case, but it becomes tricky when it comes to special cases. I know such a special case: a young mother, Austrian, resident in Vienna, food point in Vienna, single parent; Father from Slovenia and hold father on paper. The young mother has worked fully for six years and used the pregnancy to complete her apprenticeship, which is why she does not have an employment relationship, and now Slovenia should be responsible for family benefits. In practice, this young mother has happened: several visits to the District Hospital, a trip to Slovenia with a two-month-old child, unpleasant conversations, ignorance of the staff: Well, because of you, we will not break EU law! In the meantime, five months have passed without results. The People's Procuratorate describes the ideal case, however: According to the EU Commission, the waiting period should last a maximum of two months, in any case after two months the country of residence should jump in, advance benefits and ensure that the application is forwarded to the competent country. The Public Procuratorate describes some cases of this kind and has also recommended that training be carried out by the authorities or the respective bodies. This need is given, in my opinion, in the light of the case described. I'm appealing to you: So one cannot jump around with a young mother who has chosen to be a single parent for her child. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] It has come to this illustrious situation, trusting in our social system. I hope that my speech can help to ensure that this case finally comes to a good end and that the necessary recommendations of the public prosecutors are taken seriously, because the problematic social situation for this young mother should have been solved for a long time. The mother concerned has already addressed the public prosecutor's office, and unfortunately her case will appear in the 2017 report. Of course, I thank you very much for recording this so that you continue to work on it and really are there for the people and for the people. Thank you.
